通膨升溫壓抑美股表現，近期聚焦財報結果
2026 年 10 月 15 日
一、 市場動態
1. 科技股領跌，費半連 6 日收黑：美股主要指數 10 月 14 日全數下跌，道瓊工業指數下
跌 0.09%，收 51,621.48 點；S&P 500 指數下跌 1.11%，收 7,718.94 點，為 7 月 29
日以來最大單日跌幅；Nasdaq 綜合指數下跌 1.88%，收 26,692.87 點，為 7 月 23
日以來最大單日跌幅。費城半導體指數下跌 4.30%，收 11,684.38 點，連續第６個交
易日走低，為 2025 年 5 月以來最長連跌紀錄。

2. 類股非全面下挫，資金仍呈輪動現象：半導體及 AI 相關股票在 10/14 跌幅尤其明顯
，AMD 下跌 5.73%、Nvidia 下跌 3.16%、Broadcom 下跌 3.10%，AI 基礎建設相關
的 CoreWeave、Nebius、Oracle 及 Dell 跌幅更深，顯示市場對資本支出規模較大、
融資成本敏感度較高的 AI 相關企業風險偏好明顯下降。不過，S&P 500 的 11 大類
股中有 8 類下跌，資訊科技及通訊服務為主要拖累來源，但醫療保健、必需消費與能
源類股仍逆勢上漲。
3. 美國 10 年期公債殖利率盤中急升後回落：美國 10 年期公債殖利率 10/14 由 5.2%左
右快速升至約 5.33%，逼近近期高點；然而，隨後市場消化 Fed 官員較審慎的政策訊
號，殖利率自高檔回落，終場約收於 5.19%，較前一交易日 5.21%小幅下降約 2 個基
點。
二、 下跌原因分析
1. 美 9 月通膨高於預期，Fed 再升息風險重新升高：美國 10/14 公布 9 月 CPI 月增 0.6%

、年增率也由 8 月的 3.4%上揚至 3.7%；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%、年增率由 8 月的 2.4%
升至 2.6%。其中汽油價格單月大漲 9.1%，貢獻整體 CPI 月增幅超過一半；住宅成本
亦月增 0.4%，顯示物價壓力仍具黏性。此數據公布後，市場重新提高 Fed 進一步升
息的預期，CME FedWatch 所反映的 10 月升息 1 碼機率一度由前一日約 22%提高至
37%。雖然截至 10/15 台北時間早上 7 點左右該機率回降至 29.3%，這也提醒投資人
Fed 的升息路徑仍存在不確定性。
2. 高利率同時壓縮科技股估值與提高 AI 融資成本：科技及半導體公司未來獲利成長占評
價的比重較高，因此對折現率變化相對敏感。當長天期美債殖利率維持 5%以上，市
場願意支付的本益比自然受到限制，即使企業獲利仍維持成長，股價也可能因評價倍
數下降而出現修正。另一方面，本輪 AI 投資需要大量資料中心、伺服器、電力與網路
基礎設施，高利率亦直接增加企業融資成本，反映信用市場對高度依賴外部資金的 AI

基礎建設公司要求更高風險溢酬。

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
3. AI 投資焦點由「支出規模」轉向「投資報酬率」:近期市場對 AI 的疑慮逐漸由「需求
是否成長」，轉向「龐大的資本支出能否轉化為足夠營收、獲利與自由現金流」。
Broadcom 執行長陳福陽近期亦指出，AI 未來發展的重要限制可能逐漸由晶片或記憶
體供應轉向資本本身。我們認為，這並不代表 AI 資本支出週期即將結束，而是市場已
由第一階段的「資本支出愈大、成長想像愈高」，逐步進入更重視資本效率與投資報酬
率的第二階段。未來財務結構、現金流與 AI 商業化能力，將成為不同企業評價分化的
重要因素。
4. 中國記憶體競爭疑慮加重半導體短線壓力：中國長鑫存儲（CXMT）10/10 傳出記憶

體技術取得進展，引發市場對未來 DRAM 供給增加及價格競爭的疑慮，記憶體相關類
股率先承壓，並進一步影響整體半導體市場情緒。惟 DRAM 與 GPU、客製化 ASIC、
先進晶圓製程及先進封裝的產業競爭與需求結構並不相同。我們認為，儘管記憶體競
爭升溫值得關注，但目前尚不足以延伸為整體 AI 半導體需求同步轉弱。
5. 高檔籌碼加速獲利了結：AI 與半導體仍是 2026 年美股表現最強勢的投資主題之一，
部分科技股今年累計漲幅已相當可觀。當通膨、殖利率及 AI 投資報酬疑慮同時升高，
先前漲幅較大、投資人部位較集中的族群自然成為優先調節對象。因此，本波費半快
速修正除了總經與基本面疑慮外，亦包含籌碼降溫及風險部位收縮因素，使短期價格
波動幅度可能高於企業基本面實際變化。
三、市場看法
1. 類股輪動明顯，尚非全面看空美股：科技與 AI 相關股票 10 月 14 日明顯承壓，但醫
療保健、必需消費與能源類股仍逆勢上漲，道瓊工業指數僅下跌 0.09%。此外，S&P
500 成分股下跌家數與上漲家數之比約為 1.1：1；相較於指數下跌 1.11%，市場廣度

並未同步大幅惡化。這反映資金主要由高評價且前期漲幅較大的科技與 AI 族群，轉向
現金流較穩定的防禦型產業。現階段市場特徵較接近類股輪動與風格切換，而非無差
別的全面風險撤離，亦尚未看到美股企業獲利或經濟基本面全面反轉的明確訊號。
2. 美股科技股財報前風險降載，Q3 仍存在重新定價空間：近期科技股修正，費城半導
體指數連續６個交易日下跌，也可能部分反映第三季財報季正式展開前，投資人在股
價先前漲幅較大、評價偏高及利率環境不確定下，提前降低風險曝險。真正決定後續
市場方向的關鍵，仍在於實際企業獲利能否支持目前的高成長預期。根據 Bloomberg
季度獲利成長追蹤資料，今年 Q1 與 Q2 財報季期間的 EPS 成長率經驗顯示，隨實際
財報陸續公布，企業獲利成長曾明顯優於財報季開始前的市場預期。儘管這並不保證
Q3 將複製前兩季的上修幅度，但目前市場因財報不確定性所反映的風險折價仍存在
被實際財報重新驗證的可能。若大型科技及半導體公司後續營收、獲利與 AI 需求指引
實際公布結果優於預期，近期風險溢價可望部分收斂；反之，若獲利成長無法跟上目

前評價，市場整理時間則可能進一步延長。

不另行通知。
3. 費半修正幅度大，但 AI 需求尚未出現全面反轉：ASML 在 10/14 所公布的最新第三
季財報反而顯示 AI 相關設備需求仍相當強勁，該公司表示 2027 與 2028 年規劃產能
可能仍無法完全滿足客戶需求，部分客戶亦已表達對 2029 及 2030 年設備需求的興
趣。儘管費城半導體指數近期市場情緒轉弱，但由 ASML 的財報表現來看，目前仍未
看到大型 AI 客戶全面取消晶片訂單、主要雲端服務商同步削減 AI 資本支出，或先進
製程需求快速降溫等結構性訊號。
4. 台積電營收顯示 AI 需求暢旺，留意 10/15 法說會內容：台積電 10/8 公布的第三季營

收達新台幣 1.49 兆元、年增約 50%，創歷史新高，並高於市場原先預估的 1.46 兆元
；9 月單月營收年增 54.6%。強勁的營收表現仍反映 AI 與先進製程需求具有支撐。目
前市場因此呈現「企業營收仍快速成長，但股票評價下降」的狀況。高殖利率環境下
，EPS 上升與本益比下降可以同時發生，後續股價方向將取決於企業獲利上修幅度是
否足以抵銷評價收縮。該公司將於 10/15 下午舉行第三季法人說明會。市場重點將集
中於第四季展望、AI 與 HPC 需求、先進製程、先進封裝及未來資本支出規劃。公司
對 AI 需求與產能投資的最新判斷，將成為判斷近期費半修正主要屬於評價調整，或產
業基本面是否開始出現變化的重要依據。
5. AI 與半導體進入基本面分化階段：我們認為，在高利率環境下，高度仰賴外部融資、
自由現金流尚未成熟的 AI 基礎建設業者，受到的資金成本壓力明顯高於具穩定現金流
的大型科技與雲端服務商。同樣地，記憶體、GPU、ASIC、先進製程及先進封裝各有
不同供需結構。我們預期下一階段 AI 行情將更加重視訂單能見度、產品競爭力、毛利

率、自由現金流及 AI 營收實際兌現，產業內部強弱分化程度可能進一步提高。
6. Fed 路徑仍存不確定性，惟企業獲利可望提供股市支撐：美國 9 月 CPI 升溫使市場重
新評估 Fed 後續升息時點與幅度，美國利率政策仍存在不確定性，長天期美債殖利率
也可能維持高檔。不過，現階段企業獲利成長與 AI 相關需求仍具韌性，若第三季財報

延續前兩季優於預期的趨勢，企業 EPS 若能上修有望部分抵銷高利率對本益比的壓縮
效果。換言之，後續市場表現未必只由 Fed 政策主導，企業實際獲利能否持續兌現，
將是支撐科技與半導體股中期走勢的重要力量。
四、投資建議
1. 短線聚焦美股財報與 Fed 決策，科技股中期趨勢料不致有變：近期市場同時面臨財報
前的觀望態勢、美債殖利率偏高及 AI 資本支出疑慮等不確定因素，短線風險偏好可能
受到壓抑，後續宜關注 10 月美股財報對企業獲利與資本支出展望，以及 FOMC 會議
所釋出的政策訊號。中期而言，目前 AI 相關企業營運與資本支出尚未出現全面轉弱跡

象，科技產業基本面仍具一定支撐，但在利率維持高檔及市場評價偏高的環境下，後
續宜更加重視企業獲利、現金流及資本支出的實際變化。
2. 中天期債券風險報酬仍較均衡：美國 9 月 CPI 升溫使短端殖利率持續受到 Fed 政策路
徑影響，而 20～30 年超長端仍面臨財政赤字、國債供給與期限溢價偏高等結構性壓
力。相較之下，目前殖利率已位於歷史相對高檔，若後續核心通膨或能源價格改善，
中段殖利率具有回落條件，同時其存續期間風險又低於超長端，因此現階段以中天期
美國公債及投資級債的風險報酬結構相對較佳。

Document 055: 20261117 焦點專題：從管晶片到管算力

• 報告日期：2026年11月17日
BIS新規將遠端算力納入監管
本報告僅供內部使用
從管晶片到管算力

### Page 2

2
【結論摘要】
四大新規總結
資料來源：U.S. Department of Commerce, BIS, RIN 0694-AK50 IFR（預公告版）；台新投顧整理
① 資料中心算力管制｜新 §744.24
資料中心若對總部位於 D:5 或澳門的企業提供逾 1,200 萬 TPP 算力（約
760 顆 H100），出口相關晶片須申請許可；商業推薦系統經申報後可豁免。
② 東南亞四國＋巴西目的地許可｜§742.6
出口、再出口或境內移轉先進 AI 晶片至印尼、馬來西亞、新加坡、泰國及
巴西，均須申請許可；客戶總部在美國者免許可，但須按月申報。
③ 代工／封測推定收緊｜3A090.a Note 1
設計公司位於或母公司在 D:1、D:4、D:5 或五國者，代工／封測廠不能再
靠電晶體數量推翻「屬 3A090.a」推定。
④ 正式廢除 AI Diffusion Rule
取消國家分層、全球 RS 許可、AIA／LPP 例外、Data Center VEU 與模型
權重管制；保留 ACM 例外與 FDP 規則。

重點：管制範圍從晶片銷售擴大到算力租用。出口時若「知情」，海外資料中心把算力租給中資，出口商也要負責。東南亞轉運熱點直接列入許
可範圍，美系雲端業者相對有利。
說明｜D:5：美國武器禁運國家組（含中國、俄羅斯）；TPP（Total Processing Performance）：衡量晶片運算效能的指標，數值越高效能越強；RS：區域
穩定管制理由；VEU（Validated End-User）：經 BIS 事先核准、可免逐筆申請許可的最終用戶；3A090.a：最高等級的先進運算晶片（如 H100、B200）；
OSAT：委外封裝測試廠；AIA／LPP／ACM：免申請許可的例外通道。

### Page 3

3
【新舊架構對照】
管制架構從「全球配額」轉為「目的地＋用途」審查
資料來源：BIS, RIN 0694-AK50 IFR（預公告版）前言第 I、IV 節；台新投顧整理
面向 AI Diffusion Rule（2025/1，未執行） 本次 IFR
國家架構 三級國家分層＋全球算力配額 取消分層；D:1／D:4／D:5、UAE 及五國須許可，其餘國家無國別許可
遠端算力 未直接規範，僅以政策聲明警示 新 §744.24：對 D:5 企業提供逾 1,200 萬 TPP 算力即須許可
資料中心授權 Universal／National VEU 取消 Data Center VEU，回歸一般 VEU 授權
模型權重 ECCN 4E091＋模型權重 FDP 規則 全數移除
許可例外 AIA、LPP、ACM 刪除 AIA、LPP；保留 ACM（支援非 D:5 終端供應鏈）
監控方式 配額＋VEU 安全標準 個案許可＋美系豁免月報＋KYC 查核（不配合即推定知情）＋用途變更
通知
說明｜三級分層：舊制將各國分為盟友（不設限）、多數國家（設配額）與受管制國家（禁止）三級；FDP：外國用美國技術生產的產品也受美國管轄；

VEU：經 BIS 事先核准、可免逐筆申請許可的最終用戶；許可例外（AIA／LPP／ACM）：符合條件即可免申請許可的通道；D:1／D:4：國安、飛彈技術顧
慮國家組。

【新規一｜§744.24】
資料中心遠端算力管制：四個條件同時成立即須許可
資料來源：BIS, RIN 0694-AK50 IFR §744.24(a)–(d)；台新投顧整理
出口、再出口或境內移轉時，若「知情」（knowledge）有下列情形，須取得 BIS 許可；採個案審查，且不適用任何許可例外
A｜產品
3A090（不含 .c HBM）、4A090
及 .z 項目，含 AI 伺服器、相關
軟體與技術
B｜地點
位於全球任何地點的資料中心，
包括以實體或數位方式串連運作
的 linked data centers
C｜規模
提供逾 12,000,000 aggregate
TPP 的算力存取，單一或串連資
料中心合併計算
D｜對象
總部或最終母公司位於 D:5 或澳
門的企業，以及代其訓練或提供
模型的非 D:5 中介業者
豁免：商業推薦系統
僅限排序、篩選及推薦商品或內容的系統（如電商、社群平台），具生成
文字、影像、影音或程式碼能力者不適用。須向 BIS 申報企業名稱、TPP

總量及盡職調查，資料轉交最終用戶審查委員會（End User Review
Committee, ERC）。
既有業者：90 天過渡
規則公布前已對 D:5 企業提供大量算力的資料中心，如欲繼續服務，須於
生效後 90 天內取得許可，否則後續出口將受限制。
說明｜知情（knowledge）：知道或有理由知道；再出口（reexport）：貨品由第三國再出口；境內移轉（transfer in-country）：在同一國內轉手或變更
用途；aggregate TPP：所有晶片算力加總；linked data centers：網路串連、一體運作的多座資料中心；ERC：負責實體清單增刪的跨部會委員會。

### Page 5

5
【算力門檻】
1,200 萬 aggregate TPP 有多大？約 4 櫃GB200 NVL72
資料來源：BIS IFR §744.24、§772.1、3A090；晶片換算依 NVIDIA 公開密集 FP8 規格，台新投顧推估
TPP 估算
TPP ＝ 2 × MacTOPS × 運算位元長度
取原廠宣稱的最高值；稀疏與密集並存時，以密集（dense）計算。
Aggregate TPP：一組 IC 或設備 TPP 的加總（本規則新增於 §772.1）。
例：H100 FP8 密集 1,979 TFLOPS × 8 位元 ≈ TPP 15,832
計算範圍：單一資料中心，或以網路串連、共享資源的 linked data
centers 合計。
≈760
顆 H100／H200
（單顆 TPP ≈ 15,832）
≈330
顆 B200
（單顆 TPP ≈ 36,000）
≈4 櫃
GB200 NVL72
（每櫃 72 顆，TPP ≈ 288 萬）
解讀：門檻不高。有規模的模型訓練或生成式 AI 推論租用，幾乎都會超過；零星的雲端使用不受影響。

說明｜MacTOPS：每秒兆次「乘加」運算數；位元長度：數字精度，FP8＝8 位元；密集運算：不計稀疏化加速的實際算力；TFLOPS／PFLOPS：每秒兆次
／千兆次浮點運算；NVL72：NVIDIA 單櫃搭載 72 顆 GPU 的機櫃系統。

符合，B200 超出）；其餘 D:5 交易採推定拒絕。
說明｜ECCN：出口管制分類編號；3A090：先進運算晶片，4A090：內含這類晶片的電腦，.z：內含這類晶片的其他設備；性能密度（performance
density）：每平方毫米晶粒的運算效能，避免以加大晶片面積規避門檻；HBM：高頻寬記憶體；FDP：外國製造但用美國技術生產的產品也受美國管轄；
推定拒絕（presumption of denial）：原則上不核准。

合規責任延伸至資料中心與後續每一手持有者
資料來源：BIS IFR §744.24 Notes 1–3、(a)(2)、(e)；前言第 III.B 節；台新投顧整理
KYC 盡職調查（Note 3）
出口商須向資料中心取得 KYC 資訊，確認客戶
是否由 D:5 或澳門企業直接或間接持有、控制；
資料中心拒絕或無法提供，即構成警訊（red
flag），推定出口商知情。
持有人責任（Note 1）
禁令適用於所有目前持有晶片者，不限於原始
出口交易的當事人；二手轉售及轉租同樣受規
範。
不適用微量原則（Note 2）
美國原產晶片併入外國產品後，只要供應鏈任
一環節知情將用於禁止用途，即不得適用微量
原則（de minimis）。
用途變更通知｜§744.24(e)
免許可出貨時，出口方須書面通知收貨方：日
後轉供 D:5 企業且超過門檻，須另取得授權；
用途由允許轉為禁止，視同境內移轉。
個別通知機制（is informed）｜(a)(2)
BIS 可透過個別通知或修訂法規，指定特定對

象須申請許可；國務卿或國安顧問通報高風險
地點後，商務部可於 10 天內決定是否施加許
可要求。
既有規定釐清｜§744.23
BIS 重申，D:5 企業為直接或間接採購方，或為資
料中心專屬／主要（逾 50% 算力）遠端使用者時，
即須申請許可；違規者應依 §764.5 自願揭露。
說明｜警訊（red flag）：顯示交易可能有問題的可疑跡象，出口方須先釐清才能出貨，否則視為知情；微量原則（de minimis）：外國產品中美國成分低
於一定比例即不受 EAR 管轄；自願揭露（voluntary self-disclosure）：主動向 BIS 申報違規，可望減輕處罰。

資料來源：BIS IFR §742.6、§743.10、摘要與前言第 II 節；台新投顧整理
印尼
馬來西亞
新加坡
泰國
巴西
規則摘要另稱：商務部長可會同
國務卿或國安顧問增列國家
• 管制範圍：3A090（不含 .c）、4A090、.z 項目及相關軟體、技術，出口、再出口與「境內移轉」均須許可。
• 審查政策：個案審查，評估該國出口管制制度有效性，以及與 BIS 執法合作的程度。
• 美系豁免：最終收貨人或使用者的總部或最終母公司在美國 → 免許可，但出口方須每月向 BIS 申報交易明細（§743.10，
本規則新增），內容包括各方名稱與最終母公司、最終目的地、ECCN、品名／SKU、TPP、數量與出貨日，次月首個營業
日前提交；未申報即不適用豁免。
• 國家層級措施：對未有效防堵規避的國家，考慮進口限制、新增出口許可、下調出口管制待遇，以及將相關人士列入
Entity List 或發布暫時拒絕出口令（TDO）。

說明｜轉運（diversion）：晶片先出口至第三國，再轉手至受管制國家；最終收貨人／最終用戶（ultimate consignee／end user）：實際收貨與使用晶片
的公司；實體清單（Entity List）：列入者出口須許可，且多半不獲核准；暫時拒絕出口令（TDO）：可立即停止特定對象的出口權利。§743.10：本規則新
增的申報條文，美系客戶免許可的前提是出口方按月申報；RS（Regional Stability）：區域穩定管制理由，本規則改為僅適用於五國。

移除
• 三級國家分層與全球 Regional Stability 許可要求
• License Exception AIA（§740.27）與 LPP（§740.29）
• Data Center VEU（Universal／National VEU）及 supp. 10 to part
748
• 模型權重管制：ECCN 4E091、Red Flag 28、模型權重 FDP 規則
§734.9(l)
• §772.1「Model Weights」定義
保留／重申
• License Exception ACM（§740.28）：支援終端在 D:5／澳門以外的供

意涵：美國閉源模型部署到海外，不再受模型權重管制。中東等五國以外的市場改為個案許可，算力配額上限取消。
說明｜許可例外：符合條件即免逐筆申請許可；AIA／LPP：舊制給盟友與小量出口的例外；ACM：供製造組裝用的例外；模型權重：AI 模型訓練出的參數
，等同模型本體；FDP：外國用美國技術生產的產品也受管。

### Page 12

12
【政府如何監管】
監管方式：事前許可、事中申報、事後追溯與國家層級措施
資料來源：BIS IFR §742.6、§743.10、§744.24、摘要；台新投顧整理
事前｜許可審查
• §744.24 與五國個案許可
• D:5 推定拒絕、D:1／D:4 推定核
准
• BIS 個別通知（is informed），
指定對象或地點須許可
事中｜申報與 KYC
• 美系豁免按月申報（§743.10）
• 推薦系統須申報，資料轉交 ERC
• 資料中心須配合 KYC，拒絕即推
定知情
事後｜追溯
• 用途變更須授權，視同境內移轉
• 任一手持有人皆受禁令拘束
• 紀錄保存、自願揭露與執法
國家層級
• 新增指定高轉運風險國
• 進口限制、下調管制待遇
• Entity List、暫時拒絕出口令
監管架構：BIS 不直接管雲端服務，而是從出口環節下手：以出口時是否知情為基礎，再加上持有人責任與用途變更責任。結果是晶片供應商、
伺服器廠與資料中心必須互相查核，部分執法工作等於交給供應鏈與所在國政府。

說明｜ERC（End User Review Committee）：商務、國務、國防、能源部組成的跨部會委員會，決定實體清單增刪；推定知情：資料中心不配合查核時，
視同出口方知情；境內移轉：同國內轉手或改變用途；下調管制待遇：將該國改列至管制較嚴格的國家組；TDO：暫時拒絕出口令。

說明｜Federal Register：美國聯邦公報，法規刊登後才正式生效；在途貨物緩衝條款（savings clause）：刊登當日已在運送途中的貨物，可依舊規定完成
交貨；意見徵詢（public comment）：暫行最終規則（IFR）先生效，事後徵詢意見並可能修正；AI Diffusion Rule：拜登政府於 2025 年初發布、以國家
分級與配額管制 AI 晶片的規則。

【影響評估】
資料來源：台新投顧觀點
管制重心從「晶片賣給誰」
延伸到「算力租給誰」
出口商只要「知情」，海外資料中心把算力租給中資，就可能違反出口管制；
中資經東南亞租用算力的管道將明顯收窄；美系雲端在五國免許可，競爭位置反而變好。

【影響（一）｜產業】
中資租戶與東南亞 AIDC 受壓，美系雲端相對受惠
資料來源：BIS IFR；台新投顧整理與推估，影響方向為定性判斷，非個股投資建議
產業／代表公司 影響 主要邏輯
NVIDIA、AMD 中性偏負 東南亞中資租用需求受限，出貨文件變多、交期拉長；美系雲端需求不受影響，對中國出口
H200 的個案審查管道也還在
AI 伺服器 ODM（鴻海、廣達、緯穎、緯
創、英業達）
中性，交期風險 出貨五國須許可或按月申報，合規成本與交期不確定性上升；ACM 例外保留，供應鏈轉運影
響有限
東南亞資料中心營運商／neocloud 負面 中資的訓練與生成式租戶須許可，且恐難獲准；KYC 成本上升，以中資客戶為主的擴建計畫可
能延後
美系雲端（微軟、Google、亞馬遜） 正面 總部在美國，免許可；中資退出五國後留下的需求，最可能由它們承接
中國 AI／雲端（字節跳動、阿里巴巴、
騰訊）
負面 海外租用算力的管道受限；生成式 AI 不適用推薦系統豁免，預期加速轉向國產晶片，並申請

H200 許可
台積電、日月光等代工封測 中性 營收影響有限，但客戶篩選與合規成本上升；中國及東南亞背景的設計客戶只能走途徑 a 證明
說明｜AIDC：AI 資料中心；neocloud：專門出租 GPU 算力的新興雲端業者，如 CoreWeave；ODM：替品牌代工設計與製造的廠商；ACM 例外：為製造
、組裝目的移轉晶片可免許可；途徑 a：由核准／授權設計公司出具算力證明；中性偏負：整體影響不大，但方向偏不利。

東南亞 AIDC 納入許可管制，區域投資須重新評估客戶結構

柔佛 AIDC 受影響最大，中資租戶多的業者面
臨許可與 90 天期限。大馬 2025/7 已自設 AI
晶片出口許可，執法合作將左右審查寬嚴。

過去多起輝達 GPU 轉運執法案件都經過新馬。
中資雲端業者在當地的據點，客戶與資料中心

媒體報導泰國是中資租用算力的管道之一。泰

較寬鬆的審查。

AIDC 投資才剛起步就被納入管制。美系雲端
專案照常推進，只是須按月申報；中資案件的
審批不確定性升高。
巴西
五國中唯一不在亞洲的國家，可見美方已把拉
美 AIDC 視為轉運風險。當地資料中心投資案
若有中資客戶，須重新盤點。
中東（UAE 等）

不在五國名單內。UAE 核准實體免許可，D:1
／D:4 推定核准，處境相對有利，可能承接部
分轉移需求。
說明｜柔佛（Johor）：馬來西亞南部、緊鄰新加坡的資料中心聚落；轉運執法案件：美國起訴經第三國轉運輝達晶片的案件；審查待遇：BIS 審許可時對該
國的寬嚴程度；轉移需求：受限的算力需求改往其他地區；推定核准：申請原則上會被批准。

### Page 17

17
【影響（三）｜算力：計算說明】
新規約影響 2027 全球新增算力 2–3.5%，上限約 7%
資料來源：IFP（2026）、Epoch AI（Chip Sales、Chip Smuggling，2026）；台新投顧整理與假設
STEP 1｜分母：全球算力
2026 年底全球存量：約 4,600 萬
H100e（Epoch AI）
2027 年全球新增：假設 4,000 萬（產
能大幅開出）
2027 年底存量：4,600 ＋ 4,000 ≈
8,600，取整約 9,000 萬
→ 新增 4,000 萬／存量 9,000 萬
STEP 2｜分子：遠端存取
IFP 估計：中資遠端租用算力：今年約
100 萬 → 2027 年底 280 萬
交叉驗證：880 萬（IFP預估中國可取得
總算力）× 65%（尚未取得、 2027 年
才到位）× ½（遠端）≈ 285 萬
2027 年新增：280 萬 − 100 萬（今年
已在使用）＝ 180 萬
→ 受影響增量約 180 萬
STEP 3｜加上走私

走私：Epoch 估計至 2025 年底累計約
66 萬（區間 29–160 萬）
假設：2027 年單年走私 30–100 萬（台
新假設）
計算：低情境下 180 ＋ 30 ＝ 210 萬；
高情境下 180 ＋ 100 ＝ 280 萬
→ 毛額 210～280 萬
STEP 4｜扣掉轉單（淨額）
需求會轉向：推薦系統豁免、個案許可
、H200、國產晶片、非中資租戶
假設：50–60% 轉向其他管道，實際流
失 40–50%
計算：210 × 40% ≈ 85；280 × 50% ＝
140
→ 淨額 85～140 萬
STEP 5｜換算占比＝分子 ÷ 分母
分子（萬 H100e） ÷ 全球新增 4,000 萬 ÷ 全球存量 9,000 萬 ÷ NVIDIA 2,400 萬*
遠端存取新增量 180 4.5% 2.0% 7.5%
含走私量 210–280 5.3–7.0% 2.3–3.1% 8.8–11.7%
扣除轉單（淨額）85–140 2.1–3.5% 0.9–1.6% 3.5–5.8%

* NVIDIA 新增＝4,000 萬 × 假設市占 60%；遠端存取幾乎都是 NVIDIA 晶片
說明｜IFP：美國智庫 Institute for Progress；Epoch AI：追蹤全球 AI 晶片與算力的研究機構；分子：受新規影響的算力，分母：比較基準；65%、½ 為 IFP 對中國算力來源的拆分。
4.5%  遠端存取增量占 2027 全球新增算力
5–7%  加計走私曝險上限
2–3.5%  扣除轉單後的淨影響

名詞定義
資料來源：BIS IFR §744.24(d)、§772.1、ECCN 3A090 Technical Notes；台新投顧整理
名詞 定義（依 IFR 及 EAR）
Country Group D:5 美國武器禁運國家組，含中國、俄羅斯等；本規則另將澳門比照處理
TPP Total Processing Performance＝2 × MacTOPS × 運算位元長度，取最高值、以密集運算計
Aggregate TPP 一組 IC 或設備 TPP 的加總（新增於 §772.1）
Linked data centers 以網路基礎設施串連、可共享資源、平衡負載並複製資料而整體運作的多個資料中心，含數位連結
Commercial recommender system 專為且僅用於依使用者行為、交易紀錄或品項屬性排序、篩選、推薦商品或內容的 AI 工具（原文稱 SI）；任何生成
式系統或內含生成模型者均排除
知情（knowledge） EAR 用語，含知道或有理由知道；資料中心拒絕提供 KYC 資訊即構成警訊（red flag），推定知情

Front-end fabricator／OSAT 前段晶圓製造廠／委外封裝測試廠
Ultimate parent 最終母公司；管制以總部或最終母公司所在地判定，不論子公司註冊地

【附錄】
中英名詞對照
資料來源：BIS IFR、EAR（15 CFR Parts 730–774）；台新投顧整理
中文（英文原文） 白話說明
知情（knowledge） 知道或有理由知道交易涉及禁止用途；對明顯疑點刻意不查，也算知情
推定（presumption） 先認定屬某種情況，除非能提出反證
推定拒絕／推定核准（presumption of denial／approval） 許可申請原則上不核准／原則上核准
個案審查（case-by-case review） 無預設立場，逐案判斷是否核准
警訊（red flag） 顯示交易可能有問題的可疑跡象；出口方須先釐清才能出貨
個別通知（is informed） BIS 以書面通知特定業者，指定某些交易須申請許可
再出口（reexport） 貨品由第三國再出口至另一國
境內移轉（transfer in-country） 在同一國內轉手，或變更最終用途
最終收貨人／最終用戶（ultimate consignee／end user） 實際收貨／實際使用貨品的對象

微量原則（de minimis） 外國產品所含美國成分低於一定比例，即不受 EAR 管轄
許可例外（License Exception） 符合特定條件，即可免逐筆申請許可
外國直接產品規則（Foreign Direct Product rule, FDP） 外國以美國技術或設備生產的產品，同樣受 EAR 管轄
在途貨物緩衝條款（savings clause） 規則刊登時已在運送途中的貨物，可依舊規定完成交貨
自願揭露（voluntary self-disclosure） 主動向 BIS 申報違規，可望減輕處罰
暫行最終規則（Interim Final Rule, IFR） 刊登後即生效，事後再徵詢意見並可能修正的法規

### Page 1

M
Update
Compal Electronics  | Asia Pacific
Server Ramp Takes Shape but
Valuation Keeps Us UW
Compal’s server expansion is progressing, with revenue
contribution rising to ~12% in 3Q and management targeting 30-
40% in CY27. However, weaker PC volumes and diminishing
scale benefits continue to pressure margins. At ~14x CY27E P/E,
valuation remains stretched.
3Q OpM missed. See our  3Q26 Results Summary                   .
PC business facing demand and profitability headwinds: Compal expects a mid-

teens q/q decline in 4Q26 shipments because of higher selling prices. This weakness
should persist into CY27; the company projects a likely double-digit y/y unit decline,
though it admitted that visibility remains low. However, rising ASPs from mix
changes and cost pass-through could keep 4Q PC revenue up sequentially.
Servers have become a key growth driver: Servers rose from ~5% of the mix in 1Q
to ~12% in 3Q, with a CY27 revenue contribution target of 30-40%. While 4Q26

server revenue is guided to be flat q/q, Compal is aggressively scaling its capacity. It
now primarily ships from Taiwan, and a major L10/11 ramp will begin in 2Q27 at its
new Texas plant. It currently does L10 HGX for Neocloud customers and L6 for a US
enterprise customer; it will do L10/11 for enterprise customer(s) as well, and is
actively approaching CSP customers for ASIC L11 solutions as well.
Margins to remain pressured: Overall margins were pressured by the PC segment,

driving a sequential decline in 3Q owing to lower PC volumes and utilization. This
margin headwind should intensify in 4Q because of shrinking PC business scale.
Conversely, server gross margin should remain resilient in the high single digits to
10%,  staying flat q/q.
4Q preview: We estimate that 4Q revenue will grow ~8% q/q, to NT$263bn (+38%
y/y), primarily driven by higher PC ASPs despite declining volumes. GM should
contracting ~10bps q/q, to 4.3% (-150bps y/y), with declining PC profitability, mainly

owing to diminishing scale benefits. This leads to our OP estimate of NT$2.8bn
(+25% q/q, +6% y/y) and NP estimate of NT$1.7bn (-34% q/q, +21% y/y), for EPS of
NT$0.39. Our 4Q net profit estimate is down q/q because of lower non-op income.
Stay UW with price target unchanged at NT$23.0 (10.4x CY27 P/E). We think
valuation looks quite stretched now at ~14x CY27e P/E. We prefer other ODMs like
Wistron (6x), Hon Hai (12x), Wiwynn (8x), or Quanta (9x).

Morgan Stanley appreciates your support in
the 2027 Extel Asia Research Survey.

+33% y/y), 15% above our estimate/2% above the Street, for EPS of NT$0.59.

### Page 4

M
Update
4
Price Target and Valuation Methodology
Our 12-month price target is unchanged at  NT$23.0: Our price target is our base case
scenario value, derived from a multistage residual income (RI) model. We use the
company's beginning equity plus the present value of all expected residual income in
excess of the cost of capital. The RI is positive when ROAE is above the cost of capital and
negative when it is below.
We assume a cost of equity of 9.1%  (beta of 1.3, equity risk premium of 6.0% and risk-free

rate of 1.5%), a medium-term growth rate of 5%, and a terminal growth rate of 3% (all
unchanged).
Our new price target implies a 2026e P/E of 10.4x and a 2027e P/E of 8.5x, below the five-
year average of 13.8x. We view these as fair, because we factor in more conservative
assumptions for its consumer business and a weaker margin outlook for Compal. The
company's venture into the "new businesses" (e.g., auto, server, etc.) is constraining its
margin profile as it tries to shift from 80%+ PC to more commercial exposure.

Our bull and bear case values are also unchanged, at NT$38.0 and NT$17.0,
respectively.

▪ Compal’s server expansion is progressing,

with revenue contribution rising to ~12% in
3Q and management targeting 30-40% in
CY27.

diminishing scale beneﬁts continue to
pressure margins.

EPS estimate, below the ﬁve-year average of
14x. We view this implied P/E as fair, because
we factor in more conservative assumptions
for its PC business and a weaker margin
outlook for Compal.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Hon Hai Precision (2317.TW)
Base case scenario value, derived from a residual income valuation model. Our key assump-
tions include a cost of equity of 8.5%, a medium-term growth rate of 13%, and a terminal
growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected iPhone sell-through
n Faster progress in AI server business
n Faster EV business development progress
n Any new M&A activity that could improve sentiment

Risks to Downside
n Lower iPhone sell-through
n Smaller profit contribution from AI business
n Slower EV business development progress
n Geopolitical developments that could negatively affect foreign investment
Quanta Computer Inc. (2382.TW)
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions include a cost of equity of 9.0% (beta of
1.2, equity premium of 6.0% and risk-free rate of 1.5%), a 7.0% medium-term growth rate, and
a 3% terminal growth rate.

Risks to Upside
n Stronger-than-expected NB demand
n Stronger-than-expected Apple Watch demand
n Stronger-than-expected server demand
n Faster-than-expected AI server penetration
Risks to Downside
n Weaker-than-expected NB demand
n Softer-than-expected Apple Watch demand
n Weak margin performance owing to rising labor costs and sales shortfalls
n Fierce price competition in the mega data center segment
n Slower-than-expected AI server penetration

Wistron Corporation (3231.TW)
Base case, residual income valuation. Key assumptions: 8.7% cost of equity, 5.0% medium-
term growth rate and 3% terminal growth rate.
Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected divestiture of consumer electronics business
n Stronger-than-expected NB demand
n Margin expansion from better product mix

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Document 154: 261113_daiwa_ Lotes

Source PDF: all report pdf/261113_daiwa_ Lotes.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We review Lotes’s 3Q26 results, released on 13 November.

What’s the impact: Business outlook. Ongoing gross margin dilution due 
to rising raw material costs and less benefits from the AI server trend has 
been, in our view, the primary drag on Lotes’s share price over this year. 
We believe this trend may reverse now as investors are worried about the 
potential AI capex cuts impacting the overall Taiwan supply chain’s revenue 
outlook in 2028, whereas Lotes only has 3-5% of exposure currently. Also, 
management expects gross margin pressure to start easing with the new 
product mass production (eg, SOCAMM) starting from 4Q26. Second, 
Lotes’s regular server-related products may see less chance for inventory 
correction as its products are not in shortage now. Lastly, its desktop 
business has suffered more than the overall industry’s headwinds (ie, unit 
decline 10-15% YoY) as its desktop products had to downgrade from DDR5 
to DDR4/3 due to component shortages, which further negatively impacted 
profitability. We believe these three headwinds will gradually transform into 
tailwinds once the AI capex starts to slow down. However, if the AI server 
capex trend remains as is, we could also see some upside for the 
company. Lotes is still eager to develop a GPU socket for the Rubin Ultra 
generation, which we believe could become a new growth driver. Besides, 
quick disconnect (QD) and NPO sockets might also become growth drivers. 
While QD only accounts for low single digit of total revenue from 3Q26, we 
expect it to have a chance to increase to c.10% of total revenue in 2027E.

3Q26 profitability missed expectations. Lotes’s 3Q26 earnings came in 
at TWD1.92bn (EPS: TWD17.1), which was 11%/14% lower than our/street 
estimates. Despite revenue largely in-line with expectations, gross margin 
decreased by 0.6pp QoQ to 44.8%, mainly from rising copper and gold 
prices; unfavourable product mix (rising high-speed cable and connect and 
QD) may be another reason. However, operating margin rose 0.9pp QoQ to 
22.9% mainly on the lack of one-off employee stock bonus expense (as 
done in 2Q26) but opex was still higher than the company’s guidance 
(<TWD2.0bn) due to salary increase for its Chinese branches.

What we recommend: We lower 2026-27E EPS by 2-7% to factor in 3Q26 
results and lower profitability recovery assumption but maintain 2028E 
EPS. We reaffirm our Buy (1) call but lower our 12M TP to TWD2,480 (from 
TWD2,850), based on a lower PER of 22x (from 28x; high end of its past-5-
year range of 10-28x), on our 1-year forward EPS, given the weaker market 
sentiment. Key risk: lower-than-expected adoption of server CPU platforms.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E revenues are 5-10% higher than the 
Bloomberg consensus, likely due to our more bullish view on QDs.

13 November 2026

3Q26 review: bottomed-out gross margin

➢ 3Q GM slightly declined on rising material cost but likely bottomed out 
➢ Rising QD, SOCAMM, NPO sockets to be key growth drivers in 2027 
➢ Reiterating our Buy (1) rating; lowering 12-month TP to TWD2,480

Source: Daiwa forecasts

Share price performance

Company profile 
Founded in 1986 and listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange in 2007, Lotes is one of the world’s leading 
connector vendors specialising in CPU socket/ILM and connectors, such as DDR, PCIe, SATA, and 
Type-C, with most of its production capacities at its Guangzhou, Zhongshan and Suzhou plants. Its 
superior manufacturing capability and strong client relationships are the key differentiators that make 
Lotes stand out from its competitors. Since the PC market became saturated from 2015, Lotes has 
focused on penetrating non-PC connector businesses, such as connectors for servers, automotive and 
industrial, and new types of cables (eg, Thunderbolt cable).

### Page 5

5

Lotes (3533 TT): 13 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 13 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Lotes’ board consists of 7 directors, including 3 independent directors. The total number of
directors is slightly lower than other listed companies in our coverage. Lotes held 8 board
meetings in 2025 and the actual attendance rate was 95%.
Although the Chairman and CEO are not the same person, they are, in fact, brothers and the co-
founders of Lotes. It gives us a sense that Lotes is still a family enterprise, which is likely a
negative for the board and executive management. That said, the background of independent
directors is diversified with expertise in technology and management. We see it as a positive for
the board's quality.
Capital management 2
Lotes declared a DPS of TWD41.5 in 2025, representing a payout ratio of 50%. Being a leading
connector vendor for applications such as desktops, notebooks and servers, Lotes grew rapidly
along with its key clients (Intel and AMD) on strong technical capabilities and higher growth rate
from the server market. The solid revenue CAGR in the past decade required higher working
capital and continuous capex to expand its capacity, which could also be the culprit for its slightly
lower payout ratio compared with other electronic component makers. Having said that, we think
Lotes’ higher revenue growth should partially offset the drawback of a lower payout ratio.
Related party &
transaction 1
In 2025, there were no disclosed related-party sales, while purchases from related parties
amounted to TWD4.4m, representing 0.03% of total cost of sales. We see limited risk from
related-party transactions.
S Supply chain management 2
Lotes established its RBA management system in 2010 and requires its suppliers to meet the
standards of no child labour, no forced labour, treating employees with respect and dignity, and
caring for labourers' safety and health. Lotes hosts a yearly supplier conference to promote the
RBA guidelines to its suppliers. In 2025, 187 suppliers participated in the annual supplier
conference. Lotes also requires its suppliers to sign the RBA-related commitment letters. For
better supply chain management, Lotes conducts yearly audits and regular business reviews
and holds the yearly supplier conference as measures. In 2025, Lotes conducted written
evaluations of 110 suppliers and on-site audits of 61 suppliers. In addition, all 76 newly added
suppliers passed its environmental and social responsibility evaluations. Lotes has required new
suppliers to sign the "Supplier Social and Environmental Responsibility Commitment Letter"
since 2010 to extend social responsibility concepts and requirements to the supply chain.
For "green" purchase management, Lotes has adopted the IECQ QC 080000 hazardous
substance process management system. Lotes asks its suppliers to follow the RoHS, HSF, and
REACH guidelines to avoid using hazardous materials. Lotes also conducts conflict minerals due
diligence and requires suppliers to sign non-conflict mineral declarations and complete surveys
tracing mineral sources to smelters. Overall, Lotes believes it has constructed a comprehensive
system covering system certification (e.g., ISO 9001), product design, supply chain
management, and production techniques to ensure that the entire manufacturing process, from
raw materials to end products, follows green principles. All products from Lotes are now 100%
compliant with RoHS standards.
S Data security 2
Lotes has established a team to implement data security regulations, planning and maintenance.
Moreover, it monitors vulnerabilities in its information systems each year through regular
vulnerability scanning, access control reviews and information security audits. The company
reported no losses resulting from information security incidents in 2025.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
Limited direct impact on Aspeed and Montage in the near term,

but we see potential long-term demand risks.
The US BIS has tightened export controls to restrict Chinese companies' remote
access to advanced computing capacity through third-country data centers, while
adding licensing requirements for advanced chips shipped to key Southeast Asian
markets, including Singapore and Malaysia (link).
Our view: Gary Yu (China Internet Analyst; link) sees this as another step toward
closing loopholes in Al compute access, potentially slowing GPU deployment by

China-linked cloud operators and raising compliance costs for overseas Al
infrastructure investment. New supplies of advanced chipsets within China to
become increasingly limited. For cloud semis, we see limited direct impact on
Aspeed and Montage in the near term but there are potential long-term demand
risks. Aspeed has a high share in GPU and China server platforms. Montage
interface and retimer chips may also be impacted over the long term.
Please also refer to our recent notes: Cloud Semis: Peak Growth, Less Upside;

Investor pushback on our cloud semis/old memory downgrades.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
Asia Pacific Summit 2026
Feedback:  Read across from Nan
Ya New Material's comments for
our coverage
Substrate CCL: Nan Ya New Material (not covered) has started shipping BT
substrate CCL in small volumes, primarily to domestic customers. We believe this is

driven by raw material supply constraints and has positive implications to non-
Japanese substrate CCL suppliers, including Elite Material, as customers diversify
away from incumbent suppliers.
Glass cloth: Nan Ya New Material expects the price trend of 7628-type glass cloth
to normalize from 2Q27 onwards, while thin/super-thin E glass cloth  remains very
under-supplied in CY27, which should provide support to thin E glass cloth pricing
through next year. The implication to Kingboard is therefore mixed.

Copper foil: Nan Ya New Material sees HTE copper foil declining while its demand
for advanced RTF and HVLP copper foils continues to surge, which bodes well for
Co-Tech, which has exited HTE copper foil business to focus on advanced RTF and
HVLP. The company also sources high-end HVLP from Co-Tech.
Customers: Nan Ya New Material mentioned major Chinese PCB vendors are all its
customers, including Shennan, WUS, Shengyi Elec, Founder, Kinwong, etc. For the

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

公司拜訪快報

融程電(3416)：國防軍工引領成長 
建議：維持買進

一、融程電為強固型電腦廠商，
近年國防軍工營收大幅提升 
 融程電成立於 1996 年，公司前三大股東為研華、醫揚、廣積。融程電為強固型
平板電腦和手持式裝置專業廠商，產品廣泛應用於航太、船舶、軍警、交通運輸、
車廠維修檢測、醫管、石化、和製程控制等利基型產業；歐洲為融程電最大市場，
賓士、保時捷、Bosch 等皆為融程電客戶，美洲市場方面，智慧倉儲解決方案廠
商 Honeywell、堆高機品牌 CROWN 以及智慧醫療 Medtronic 等亦是融程電客
戶。近年國防軍工營收大幅提升，自 2024 年的高個位數佔比提升至 2025 年的
近兩成水準，2026 年已突破兩成水準；截至 3Q26 Edge AI 佔比約 10%。

二、3Q26 毛利率低於預期，係
因出貨較多倉儲客戶產品 
 3Q26 營收 14.13 億元，截至 3Q26 產品組合：車檢 21%、倉儲物流 23%、醫
療保健 12%、其他 44%(其中超過 25%為國防軍工)；獲利方面，毛利率 36.36%，
低於預期，係因 3Q26 出貨較多低毛利率 Honeywell 倉儲物流產品，營益率
19.05%，單季稅後 EPS 2.63 元。

三、2027 年仍由國防軍工驅動主
要成長 
 3Q26 毛利率受 Honeywell 倉儲物流產品出貨較多影響，Honeywell 2026 全年
訂單金額預估 2~3 億元，2027 年 Honeywell 目標回到往年全年 6 億營收水準，
目前仍在與客戶洽談新訂單；4Q26 軍工航太及車用等出貨比重提升，預期毛利
率較 3Q26 回升；整體預期 2026 全年營收+30%YOY，其中出貨量成長 10%，
其餘 20%來自 ASP 提升(包含漲價與產品組合轉佳)。 
融程電目前 BB Ratio 1.1，訂單能見度約 6~8 個月，由於缺料狀況緩解，客戶
下單能見度恢復正常水準，預期 2027 年受惠軍工航太、無人載具及 Edge AI 需
求成長，產品組合持續優化將推升 ASP 及毛利率。產品組合方面，截至 3Q26
其他業務營收佔比 44%，2027 年佔比有望提升至 50%，主由國防軍工驅動，
2027 年國防軍工預期再成長 30~40%，甚至有望上看超過 50%成長，融程電具
全球領先海事船舶電腦技術，拓展至地面工作站及無人機整機，無人載具現有月

場復甦。公司預計將於 2027 年執行泰國工廠併購案，擴充產能以支應後續訂單
成長。

電 2026 年 EPS 9.0 元、2027 年 EPS 13.04 元，維持買進，目標價 196 元。

### Page 1

2026.10.29   
HD현대중공업  (329180)

3Q26: 조선사는 우직해야 한다  Company Brief

3분기 매출 5조 7,536억원/영업이익 8,992억원 
HD현대중공업의 2026년 3분기 매출은 5조 7,536억원/영업이익은 8,992억원으로 
컨센서스(6조 1,295억원/9,671억원) 및 당사 추정치(6조 859억원/9,679억원) 대비 
매출 및 영업이익 각각 -6.1%/-7.0%, -5.5%/-7.1%를 하회하는 실적을 기록하였다.  
조선사업부 매출이 4조 3,988억원/영업이익 6,463억원(OPM 14.7%)이며 이중 상선 
OPM은 14.7%로, 2분기 17.8%에 비해 3.1%P 하락하였으나 1분기 15.9%와 크게 
차이나지 않는 수준을 유지하면서 조업일수 및 환율 하락에도 불구하고 P-MIX및 
빈티지 개선에 따른 효과를 보여주었다. 해양사업부는 매출 3,799억원/영업이익 
1,265억원(OPM 33.3%)로 지난 분기(15.9%)보다도 훨씬 높은 영업이익을 
기록하였다. 엔진기계사업부는 매출 9,511억원/영업이익 2,299억원(OPM 24.2%)를 
기록하며 역시 견조한 실적을 기록하였다.  
일회성 이익으로 Trion 공사의 C/O 180억원, 일회성 손실로는 셧다운에 따른 
조업감소 영향 500억원 및 환율하락에 따른 이익 감소분 450억원이 있었으며 금액은 
공개되지 않았으나 임단협 잠정타결에 따른 성과급/타결금 지급에 대한 추가 충당금의 
75% 인식이 반영되었다. 알려진 일회성만 제거하더라도 실질 영업이익률은 
17%이상으로 2분기 16.4%보다 높아진 호실적이라고 할 수 있다. 특히 RUYA 공사가 
본 궤도에 오르면서 해양사업부의 이익률이 괄목할 만큼 높아졌으며 지속가능하다는 
회사의 설명이다.

투자의견 BUY 및 조선업종 TOP PICK 유지, 목표주가 530,000원 하향 
HD현대중공업의 목표주가를 530,000원으로 38.3% 하향하지만, 투자의견 BUY와 
조선업종 TOP PICK을 유지한다. 환율 하락 및 28년 하반기 엔진증설 매출확대, 
해양공사 이익률 상승 등 요인을 종합 반영하여 실적 추정치를 미세 반영하여 도출한 
2027F BPS 131,910원에 Target P/B를 기존대비 하향한 4.0배로 적용하였다. 특수선 
기대감 및 선가상승 기대감 하락에 따른 조선업종 전반의 밸류에이션 하락을 반영하여 
Target을 낮췄지만 그럼에도 조선업종 중 가장 높은 멀티플을 부여하며 Top 
Pick의견을 견지한다. 여러 악재에도 불구하고 HD현대중공업은 탄탄하다. 본업인 
상선사업부의 9월말 기준 수주량은 137.9억 달러로 목표치를 이미 초과 달성하였으며 
전사 기준으로도 수주목표 90.5%를 채워넣었다. 수주잔고 또한 상선 421억 
달러/전사 609억 달러로 각각 작년 말 대비 12.6%/13.2% 증가하였다. 해양부문과 
엔진기계부문은 조선사업부보다도 높은 이익률로 든든한 실적의 기반이 되고 있다.  
최근 HD현대그룹은 미국의 EPC강자 Kiewit과 미국 합작법인 설립에 관한 계약을 
맺고 그린필드 기반의 미국 신규 조선소 건설을 추진하고 있다. 빠르면 올해 중으로 
건립과 관련한 세부 사항이 공개될 예정이며 HD현대중공업이 중추적인 역할을 담당할 
것으로 보인다. 일찍부터 브라운필드 방식으로 접근한 동종사와 달리 다소 시간은 
걸렸지만 과감하게 결정된 투자다. 조선소 예상 준공 역시 2028년 이후로 예상되어 
당장 기업가치에 반영하기는 어려우나 엔진 증설과 함께 2028년 후에는 분명한 실적 
상향 요인이 될 것으로 예상한다. 우직하게 본업에 충실하며 신중하게 미래를 준비하는 
조선소, 귀감이다.

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상 
산업추천 투자등급  
시가총액기준 산업별 시장비중대비 보유비중의 변화를 추천하는 것임 
 Overweight(비중확대) 
 Neutral(중립) 
 Underweight(비중축소)

◼ 
 Over the past

 , India has demonstrated remarkable resilience in the face of external shocks, while its

 conomic growth is one of the highest among emerging countries  
and medium

 term forecasts are favourable
 . 
However, 
 structural 
 constraints on

 are significant.

absorb its vast labour force
 .

 needs to accelerate reforms to address

 ) 
adapting the services sector to AI
 ,

and develop its industrial sector

STRONG RESILIENCE TO EXTERNAL SHOCKS
, 
SOLID 
MACROECONOMIC FUNDAMENTALS 
◼ 
 Over the past 
 two years
 , India has demonstrated remarkable resilience in the face of external shocks, while its 
macroeconomic fundamentals have remained solid.  
◼ 
 In FY2025/2026  
(ended in March 2026)
 , India was hit by a sharp rise in US tariffs. However, thanks to supportive fiscal 
and monetary policies, 
 real GDP  
grew by 7.8% in FY2026, placing India among the fastest
 -
 growing Asian economies.  
◼ 
 For the current fiscal year, the Indian economy is facing rising energy prices 
 (
due to 
 the conflict in the Middle East and 
increased food prices 
 because 
 of 
an 
unfavourable monsoon
 ) 
as well as 
 monetary tightening. Nevertheless, despite these 
new challenges, real GDP growth is expected to exceed 7%.  
◼ 
 India is currently in a stronger position than it was in 2022 to cope with the new energy shock. The fiscal leeway to support  
the economy has increased with the decline in the fiscal deficit, and inflation is more contained.  
◼ 
 Although 
 rising  
energy prices have put pressure on external accounts, FX reserves 
 remain  
sufficient to cover the country’s 
short
 -
 term external financing needs. 
 Yet, 
downward pressures on the rupee have been 
 significant 
 and 
could persist in the 
short term
 . 
In 
H1 
2026, India’s external accounts withstood the rise in energy prices fairly well
 , notably  
thanks to the sharp increase 
in workers’ remittances. 
 T
he 
widening of the trade deficit was primarily due to the substantial increase in machinery and 
equipment imports 
 while t
 he oil trade deficit increased 
 only 
moderately, as the rise in energy prices was offset by a decline 
in import volumes. 
 However, 
in H2 2026, the oil trade deficit is expected to widen significantly.  
Additionally, large portfolio outflows have exacerbated downward pressure on the rupee, necessitating interventions by 
the central bank in foreign exchange markets and the adoption of regulatory measures to support the currency. Although 
these measures hav
 e led to a rebound in the rupee (at least temporarily), inflationary pressures, volatile portfolio 
investments, and low net foreign direct investments are 
 expected to continue 
 to put pressure on the currency.  
◼ 
 D
ownward pressures on the rupee 
 and 
 rising bond yields will reduce the government's 
 already limited 
 fiscal capacity to 
increase public investment and 
 support economic development. However, this will not undermine its ability to refinance 
its debt, given its safe structure.  
◼ 
 T
he banking sector is sufficiently robust to cope with the expected rise in credit risks.

STRONG ECONOMIC GROWTH DESPITE 
STRUCTURAL CONSTRAINTS 
◼ 
 In October 2026, the IMF revised upward 
 India’s  
medium
 -
 term growth 
 forecast 
 from 6.5% to 6.8%  
per year
 . Nonetheless, 
to achieve such strong growth performance, 
 India will have to adapt to the challenges posed by artificial intelligence  
(AI)
, 
while its infrastructure and education system are not prepared for it.  
◼ 
 Moreover, India’s development remains highly uneven. 
 The middle class is still modest
 . This is explained by a large 
informal economy, heavy concentration of employment in low value
 -
 added sectors such as agriculture, low participation 
rates among women and youth, and limited industrial development. All this, in turn, reflects India’s weak i
 ntegration into 
global value chains.  
◼ 
 India faces two key structural challenges: i
 ) adapting the services sector to AI and ii) accelerating industrial development. 
Even with progress in sectors such as smartphones and pharmaceuticals, India’s industrial base remains too small to 
absorb its vast labour force. 
 Labo
u
r market 
 rigidities  
and the mismatch between training and business needs are holding 
back both the creation of quality jobs and industrial development.  
◼ 
 Low net foreign direct investment is a 
 major  
 obstacle. While 
 India attracts 
 new 
 FDI, retention is weak
 : 
 despite 
improvements in logistics, governance issues and regulatory barriers have 
 led 
 disinvestments to triple over the past five 
years.  
◼ 
 India 
 may fail in completing its 
 transition to a higher
 -
 value, more industrialised and inclusive economy if reforms do not 
accelerate 
 significantly
 . Structural change
 s 
could lag behind pressures from its rapidly growing population and 
 fast 
global 
technological advances
 .

INDIA
’S COMPLEX POSITION 
IN INTERNATIONAL TRADE 
Low 
attractiveness  
due to unfavourable business environment and limited integration in global value chains  
◼ 
 Despite very strong growth, India's attractiveness is low relative to the size of its economy. The stock of foreign direct 
investment stood at only 15% of GDP in Q2 2026 compared to 52% of GDP in Vietnam and around 67% of GDP in Thailand 
and Malaysia.  
◼ 
 This lack of appeal can be attributed to 
 India’s still unfavo
 u
rable 
 business environment and 
 its 
 limited integration into 
global value chains. India’s global market share remain
 s 
 low (1.9% of total 
 world 
 exports  
 of goods
 )
, 
 especially in 
comparison with that of China (16.1% of total exports).  
◼ 
 India has not benefited from the AI boom
 , 
since its exports of AI
 -
 related goods remain modest (0.5% of its total exports)
 . 
However, 
 it has benefited from the “China +1” strategy adopted by 
 international 
 companies seeking to diversify their 
production sources. Smartphone exports by India have increased 6.3
 -
 fold over the past five years and accounted for almost 
6% of global exports in 2025 compared to less than 1% five year
 s 
earlier.  
To 
increase its a
 ttractiveness and develop its industrial sector, India has 
 multiplied F
 ree 
T
rade 
 A
greements  
◼ 
 Over the past 18 months, 
 India  
has signed a growing number of free trade agreements 
 – 
with the European Free Trade 
Association in October 2025, the United Kingdom in July 2025, Oman in December 2025, and the European Union in January 
2026
. This 
 should help to develop the industrial base and demonstrates the Modi government's commitment to 
accelerating India's integration into global trade.  
The sectors most likely to benefit from the recent trade agreement with the EU are electronics and semiconductors, 
pharmaceuticals and chemical
 s
, textile and renewable energy technologies. But expanding trade agreements without deep 
domestic improvements in the business environment, governance, education, and infrastructure will be ineffective. India 
will continue to struggle with investment reten
 tion. 
◼ 
 The US
 -
 India bilateral trade agreement has not yet been concluded. The two sides reached a framework for an interim 
Agreement in February 2026, under which the US would apply an 18% reciprocal tariff on Indian goods, with further 
reductions subject to the 
 successful conclusion of the agreement. As of early October, negotiations 
 are 
stalled. Key sticking 
points include market access, particularly 
 in 
India’s sensitive agricultural sector.

COUNTRY RISK PROFILE

Non transfer risk   
India’s external accounts are solid with a low external debt, moderate current account deficit and large FX 
reserves. 
 India’s net debtor position reached only 5.9% of GDP in Q2 2026. In the short term, external accounts 
are expected to deteriorate 
 given 
the rise in energy prices while downward pressures on the rupee should remain 
high.  
◼ 
 External accounts have remained solid 
 despite recent adverse shocks
 . 
 In 2025, external accounts remained solid 
despite the significant increase in US tariffs. In 
 H1 
 2026, they held up well against rising energy prices. Although the 
current account deficit is expected to widen for the current fiscal year as a whole, it should remain contained, and foreign 
exchange reserves should be more than sufficient to cover the co
 untry’s short
 -
 term financing needs. The 
 major  
causes 
for concern are the 
 structurally 
 low level of foreign direct investment (although it is rising) and the increased reliance on 
portfolio investment, which exposes the currency to greater volatility.  
◼ 
 Structural vulnerability to energy price shock
 . 
India is structurally vulnerable to an energy price shock as its 
 oil and gas  
trade deficit is relatively high (2.8% of GDP in 2025). However, in H1 2026, the current account withstood fairly well the 
rise in energy prices especially thanks to the sharp increase in workers’ remittances  
(+0.7 pp to 3.7% of GDP)
 . The trade 
deficit widened significantly but principally because of the strong increase in machinery and equipment imports rather

 Currency risk
 : 
 India is vulnerable to capital outflows and is very exposed to change in US monetary policy. 
 Its

 and fiscal policy room is narrow, 
 as India has one of the lowest tax bases

risk is limited given 
 its 
sound

is

◼ 
 In FY2026 (ended in March 2026), public finances continued to improve. 
 The central government’s fiscal deficit 
narrowed to 4.4% of GDP from 5% of GDP the previous year. 
 However, t
 his reduction stemmed primarily from a significant 
rise in dividends from the central bank. 
 G
overnment revenues remained low at  
9.6% of GDP, with tax revenues stagnant 
at 
7.6% of GDP. Besides, debt
 -
 servicing costs climbed to a peak of  
3.6% of GDP, equivalent to  
37.6% of total government 
revenue
s
. 
The fiscal position is stronger than during the 2022 energy crisis, with the general government deficit estimated 
at 
7.8% of GDP  
in FY2026, compared to  
9.5% 
in FY2021/2022.  
◼ 
 For FY2027, the initial budget targets a slight reduction in the central 
 government 
 deficit, to 4.3% of GDP. This goal may 
however 
 prove challenging due to rising subsidies amid the energy shock.  
To cushion households from the shock, the government initially 
 kept 
petrol prices 
 unchanged
 . Refining firms absorbed the 
financial burden, partially offset by lower excise duties on petroleum products. Only after regional elections in mid
 -
 May 
did the government allow a modest increase in pump prices to reflect higher international 
 prices
 . 
The government has 
also increased subsidies  
to offset the impact of rising fertiliser prices on farmers.  
Revenue losses  
—
driven by  
lower excise 
duties, reduced dividends from state
 -
 owned enterprises, and the economic slowdown
 — 
could reach  
0.5% of GDP
 , while 
the cost of 
 fertiliser subsidies 
 could 
 rise from  
0.4% to 0.8% of GDP.  
To reduce the risk of fiscal slippage, the government 
has: i) raised taxes on fuel exports, ii) capped household diesel purchases; and iii) planned cuts to certain expenditures.  
◼ 
 While the primary deficit reduction  
 target may prove difficult to achieve, 
 the surge in government bond yields  
 is 
another source of severe concern
 . 
Over the first ten months of 2026
 , 
10
-
 year bond yields rose by 80 basis points, 
 and 
further increase  
is 
expected due to 
 the central bank’s 
 monetary tightening. A  
100
-
 bp 
rise in interest rates  
could push debt  
servicing costs above  
40% of 
fiscal 
 revenue by 2029
 . This is expected to 
 increase the debt
 -
 to
-
 GDP ratio 
 gradually
 .

◼ 
 Despite the high public debt level (84.6% of GDP in FY2026), refinancing risks remain moderate. 
 The debt structure is 
relatively safe, and the government benefits from access to 
 large 
 domestic savings. In Q2 2026, 87.8% of central 
government debt was domestic
 , and 
 70.5% was market debt
 . 
It is 
 almost entirely held by domestic financial institutions 
and denominated in local currency. 
 R
efinancing risks are limited as most debt is long term (the average maturity of central 
government debt is 13.8 years). T
 -

debt. Foreign investors held only 3.1% of 
 Treasury 
 securities in Q2 2026. 
 Finally,  
borrowings 
 in foreign currency 
 accounted 
for only 3.0% of total general government debt in FY2025/2026 (
 i.e. 
2.6% of GDP).

Systemic credit risk  
The banking sector has strengthened over the past five years
 , 
as illustrated by the improvement in 
 asset  
quality 
and capital buffers. The main sources of credit risks are rising household debt, the rise in energy prices, the 
unfavourable monsoon and monetary tightening.  
◼ 
 The quality of assets has improved significantly. 
 In Q1 2026, the banking sector’s non
 -
 performing loan (NPL) ratio fell 
to a multi
 -
 decade low of 1.8%. For public banks  
(
54.4% of total loans
 )
, the NPL ratio declined to 2.1%.  
◼ 
 Capital buffers are strong. 
 While 
loan loss provisions remained modest at 75.6% of NPLs, capital buffers stayed strong. 
The Capital Adequacy Ratio (CAR) rose to 17.7% in Q1 2026, up from 17.4% a year earlier. From April 2027, Indian banks 
will adopt a new Expected Credit Loss (ECL) framewo
 rk, requiring earlier provisioning for potential loan losses. In the 
short term, higher provisioning requirements could push up lending rates and tighten credit conditions 
 while 
 triggering 
capital raising or targeted government recapitalisation. 
 I
n the medium term, the reform could strengthen banking  
sector 
resilience through earlier recognition of credit risks and more robust loss buffers.  
◼ 
 Banks’ 
 liquidity 
 is 
 comfortable
 . 
It deteriorated slightly in H1 2026 because credit growth outpaced deposit growth
 ; t
he 
liquidity coverage ratio decli
 ned to 
a still solid 
 124% 
and 
the loan
 -
 to
-
 deposit ratio increased to 83% in Q2 2026 from 79.1% 
a year earlier. RBI interventions to support the rupee 
 also 
 drained liquidity
 . Moreover
 , the deposit mix shifted from low
 -
cost current and savings accounts (CASA) to higher
 -
 cost term deposits, pressuring net interest margins. However, since 
June 2026, liquidity has improved significantly due to RBI measures to attract foreign
 -
 currency non
 -
 re
sident (FCNR) 
deposits.  
◼ 
 Profitability remains satisfactory. 
 It 
 has declined since the start of the year, primarily due to narrowing net interest 
margins, but remains satisfactory. In Q2 2026, 
 the 
 return on assets (ROA) and return on equity (ROE) stood at 1.6% and 
13.0%, respectively (IMF data).  
◼ 
 P
rivate
 -
 sector  
 debt 
 is moderate
 , 
 but its increase 
 needs to be monitored.  
 It stood 
 at 105.1% of GDP in Q1 2026 (BIS  
data
), compared to 100.1% five years earlier
 , which is 
 lower than in other emerging Asian economies.  
Non
-
 financial corporat
 e 
debt was 56% of GDP in Q1 2026. Listed firms’ financial positions continued to strengthen amid 
surging profits, improving their ability to service debt. In Q2 2026, the 
 average 
 interest coverage ratio (ICR) rose to 7.4, up 
from 6.1 a year earlier. However, the share of companies with an ICR below 1 (insufficient earnings to cover interest costs) 
increased to 18.5%. Stress remain
 s 
 concentrated in micro
 -
 enterprises, agriculture, and energy
 -
 intensive sectors (
 e.g.
, 
power, steel, cement, aviation), which are 
 the 
most exposed to oil
 -
 price shocks.  
Household leverage requires monitoring. Although still moderate, household debt increased to 49.1% of GDP in Q1 2026, 
up from 42.7% five years earlier, driven largely by non
 -
 housing retail loans (58.4% of total borrowing).  
◼ 
 Monetary 
 policy 
 tightening. 
 Due to rising  
inflation (+5.5% year
 -
 on
-
 year in September) and downward pressure on the 
rupee, the RBI raised its policy rate by 25 basis points to 5.5% in October 2026 under a "calibrated tightening" stance.  
◼ 
 T
he banking sector should remain resilient  
 despite rising risks in the short term. 
 Due to rising energy prices, a poor 
monsoon, and monetary tightening, asset quality is expected to deteriorate, particularly for low
 -
 income borrowers, micro, 
and small enterprises. 
 E
arly warning signs are emerging
 , with some 
 borrowers starting to miss payments. The stress is 
concentrated in unsecured retail loans  
(
which account for over half of fresh retail slippages
 )
. 
This could lead to new 
 NPLs 
over the coming quarters, especially as higher rates and inflation squeeze repayment capacity. Additionally, in the near 
term, capital inflows may remain weak, 
 and 
 liquidity and funding pressures could intensify.

ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROZONE
Robust growth but jobs stall and trade deficit rises. Q3 GDP growth
was confirmed at a robust 0.4% q/q, but employment was flat for the
first time since the Covid crisis. In September, intra-EU exports jum -
ped 1.7% m/m and 8.8% y/y. Stronger import growth widened the EU’s

rolling 12-month trade deficit with the rest of the world to EUR63.3bn
(0.4% of GDP). These are both indications of success of the EU’s struc -
tural efforts to boost its single market and have a more domestic de -
mand-driven growth. That said, the bilateral deficit with China reached
a new high of EUR38.9bn.
A decision on ECB leadership succession could be reached as early
as next week when EU Leaders meet on 18 November with a pac -
kage deal including Pablo Hernandez de Cos as President, a yet-to-be

named German chief economist, and former French cabinet member
Laurence Boone (currently running Santander France CIB) in charge of
market operations.
Upcoming: September industrial production (Monday); deadline for
nominations to ECB Board vacancies (which should provide clarity on
the full package including the President) and ECB Financial Stability
Review (Wednesday); Q3 negotiated wages (Friday).
France
A string of modest improvements. Unemployment edged down 0.1pp

to 8.2% in Q3, with a broadly stable activity rate. The Banque de France
business survey pointed to “moderate” October growth, driven main -
ly by manufacturing and market services. This aligns with our now -
cast of 0.2% q/q in Q4 GDP growth. Business starts increased 12% y/y
(excluding micro-entrepreneurs), while corporate bankruptcies rose
4.4% y/y over the 12 months to September, with a stable economic
weight (for more context, see our latest dashboard Business creations

and bankruptcies in France). The government brought forward the mi-
nimum-wage increase to December 1 rst and set it at 1.6%. The Court
of Auditors has published a report outlining “quickly implementable”
measures that would yield savings of EUR 32.2bn (1% of GDP) in public
finances (EUR20.9bn in spending and EUR 11.3bn in revenues), inclu -
ding a freeze on social benefits and an increase in the intermediate VAT
rate. The OAT-bund spread fell below 150 bps for the first time since

mid-October, before rising back to 154 as global yields rebounded.
Upcoming: INSEE November business survey (Friday).
Germany
Contrasted export performance. September exports rose a stron -
ger-than-expected 2.0% m/m, but imports surged 4.2%, narrowing the
trade surplus to EUR15.6bn from EUR17.9bn. The export picture was
sharply divided geographically: shipments to the EU rose 3.5% m/m,
while those to the US, China and the UK declined. Final October infla -

tion data confirmed the slowdown from preliminary estimates. Whole-
sale prices increased by 0.4, down from 1.9% in September, and in line
with market estimates.
Upcoming: November GfK Consumer Confidence (Tuesday).
Italy
Industry rebounds and trade surplus swells. Industrial production beat
expectations by rising by 1.2% m/m in September, driven by energy and
capital goods, although overall output remained 0.1% lower than a year
earlier notably owing to intermediate goods (-1.4% y/y) and consumer

goods (-3.0% y/y). On a monthly basis, chemical output contracted the
most (-8.3% m/m). The trade surplus over January-September reached
EUR36.8bn, up 37.7% YTD, as exports grew 4.7% against 2.8% for im -
ports.
Upcoming: October final HICP (Tuesday).
UNITED KINGDOM
Growth accelerates to 0.7% q/q in Q3, up from 0.5% in Q2, led by a
0.9% expansion in services. Gross fixed-capital formation increased
1.7%, while household and government consumption together rose

0.6%. Business investment grew 0.4%, confounding expectations of a
contraction. Net exports contributed negatively, however, as imports
outpaced exports.
Upcoming: September unemployment and average earnings (Tues -
day); October inflation (Wednesday); UK-EU “Reset” Summit (Friday).
UNITED STATES
Hot inflation revives hawkish Fed bets. Headline and core CPI both sur-
prised to the upside in October, with headline prices rising 0.4% m/m
and core CPI posting a third consecutive 0.3% increase, a pace incons -

istent with a return to the 2% target in the near term. Producer-price
pressures remained even stronger, with headline and core PPI reaching
6.5% and 5.0% y/y respectively (below consensus for headline, in line
for core).
Fed Governor Waller said a 50bp hike “should be considered” if core
inflation came in hot, while Dallas Fed President Logan subsequent -
ly suggested the FOMC might need to move more rapidly this winter.
The two-year Treasury yield rose more than 30bp over the week to

4.85% on 12 November, while the 10Y had its worst week since Libe -
ration Day. Meanwhile, existing-home sales fell another 5.5% m/m as
higher mortgage rates weighed on housing demand.
Upcoming: October retail sales and industrial production (Tuesday);
FOMC minutes, October building permits and housing starts (Wednes-
day).
JAPAN
Stalling growth and December BoJ hike signaled. GDP growth came
in at 0.0% q/q (-0.1% AR) in Q3. This below-consensus print ended

a run of three quarters of solid growth (consensus: 0.1%, prior: 0.4%).
Household consumption fell again (-0.3% q/q) under the combined
effect of inflation and adverse weather. Net exports also weighed on
growth due to a marked rebound in imports (2.5% q/q, prior: -1.3%).
On the bright side, business investment bounced back strongly
(1.6% q/q) after contracting in both H1 quarters.
The October Monetary Policy Meeting summary suggests policyma -
kers no longer see a need to be “overly cautious” about rate hikes,

with underlying inflation described as having “already reached 2%”
and the need to move the policy rate towards neutral now clearer.
Price pressures remain significant: PPI inflation accelerated to 7.1%
y/y in October. September real household spending nevertheless fell
2.8% m/m despite solid wage momentum.
Upcoming: October CPI inflation (Friday).

### Page 2

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.42 / 16 November 2026
The bank
for a changing

Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
EMERGING ECONOMIES
ASIA
China
Weak domestic inflation, weak domestic demand, booming external
trade. CPI inflation slowed to 0.7% y/y in October and core inflation
to 0.8%. By contrast, producer price inflation continued to rise (rea -
ching 5.7% vs. 5.0% in September, driven by energy and chips), sug -
gesting margin compression for domestic consumer-facing businesses.

In October, export growth remained exceptionally strong at 35.2% y/y in
USD terms, supported by global demand for AI-related and green-tech
products. Integrated-circuit exports surged 137.1% in value, largely re-
flecting price effects, while auto exports rose 37.6%, primarily because
of higher volumes. Imports increased 41.6%, and the trade surplus
reached USD105.3bn in October and USD1,265bn over the 12 mon -
ths to October (from 1,180bn in 2025). Industrial production growth

slowed to 4.8% y/y in October from 5.5% in September, although the
electronics sector still posted solid performance. Meanwhile, domes -
tic demand remained weak, with both consumption and investment
slowing. Retail sales increased by 0.4% y/y in nominal terms (from
1.1%) and contracted in real terms, while the fall in nominal fixed-as -
set investment continued. The loss of momentum in economic growth
was also visible in services production growth (4.6% y/y vs. 4.8%).

India
Inflation breaks through 6%. Headline CPI accelerated to 6.1% y/y
in October from 5.5% previously, moving above the RBI’s 6% ceiling for
the first time in two years. Core inflation increased to 4.5%. With the
policy rate already raised 25bp to 5.5% in October, another 25bp in -
crease is expected at the December meeting.
CENTRAL EUROPE
Mild Q3 GDP uptick and mixed inflation data. The region’s Q3 GDP
growth was slightly higher compared to the previous quarter (2.2% y/y;

2.0% in Q2), mainly driven by Poland (3.6% y/y) and to a lesser extent
by Hungary (1.5% y/y) and Czech Rep. (1.6% y/y). Romania saw a po -
sitive growth (0.3% y/y) albeit soft, after three consecutive quarters in
contraction. Meanwhile, inflation print was mixed with an increase in
Hungary (2.0% y/y), Romania (6.9% y/y) and Poland (3.5% y/y). Inflation
was softer in Czech rep. (2.4% y/y) and Slovakia (3.0% y/y). Only Hun -
gary and Poland’s inflation rates are within the central bank’s target

range. Separately, Romania’s policy rate was kept on hold at 6.5% as
expected.
LATIN AMERICA
Persistent inflation pressures. In October, inflation rose in Brazil
(4.6% y/y), Mexico (3.8% y/y), and Colombia (6.3% y/y), driven by soa -
ring food prices, exacerbated by El Niño’s impact on agricultural out -
put. In Brazil, food inflation reached 5.7% y/y, while Mexico’s non-core
food prices jumped to 13.2% y/y, and Colombia’s food prices hit 7.3% y/y,

the highest since 2023. Central banks are now expected to maintain
rates on hold for an extended period.
Brazil
Modest fiscal adjustment. On 12 November, the Senate approved the
main provisions of a fiscal adjustment package aiming for a prima -
ry surplus of 0.5% of GDP in 2027. However, the proposed measures
are modest and do not address the structural rigidity of mandatory
spending. President Lula faces a difficult balancing act of reassuring
markets and preserving social commitments.

Paraguay
Ueno Bank collapse triggers confidence crisis. Paraguay’s sixth-largest
bank, Ueno Bank, was placed into resolution on 2nd November after its
capital fell below regulatory minimums, amid concerns over improper
related-party lending. The bank served nearly 3 million customers, re-
presenting around 60% of the adult population. Its collapse triggered a
major political crisis, culminating in President Santiago Pena’s removal
from office. No systemic banking crisis is expected, as other banks re -

main solvent and liquid. However, fiscal pressures are mounting, while
political uncertainty poses downside risks to growth. On November
11th, Fitch placed Paraguay’s (BB+) rating on Negative Watch, citing
risks to depositor confidence and fiscal stability.
COMMODITIES
Oil and gas: Physical pressures ease, but geopolitical risk lingers.
Brent rose by 3.4% over the week to USD96/bbl (after having fallen
below USD90 for a short while this week on hopes Iran-Us talks were

gaining traction). Physical supply tensions continued to ease: the IEA
reported that global crude supply exceeded demand in October for the
first time since the outbreak of the war and it expects the market to re-
turn to surplus in Q4. US commercial crude inventories rose by 4.7mb,
while strategic reserves fell by 2.6mb. Nevertheless, diesel cracks have
remained almost stable over the week (0.2% in the US and 0.4% in
Europe) on the back of persisting limited exports from the Gulf and

damages on Russian refineries. European gas prices rose 8% to EUR89/
MWh amid continuing constraints on Gulf LNG exports, colder weather
and outages at Norwegian gas facilities.
TO WATCH
Trilateral Summit between presidents Putin, Trump and Xi on the side-
lines of APEC Summit (Thu).
y/y = year-on-year
m/m = month-on-month
bp = basis point
pp = percentage point

Weekly Economics
U.S. Week Ahead
Existing Home Sales • Thursday
Housing market conditions remain highly unfavorable. Mortgage
rates are the primary headwind, and they have climbed even higher
alongside the rise in longer-term yields. At the same time, low
supply is preventing a broad correction in home prices, leaving
prospective buyers squeezed on two fronts.
We expect that existing home sales slid 3.4% in August to an
annualized rate of 3.92 million. Behind this forecast is ongoing

weakness in mortgage applications and a recent bounce in
mortgage rates, which rose from 6.43% at the beginning of July to
6.66% by month-end.
We do not expect affordability conditions to meaningfully improve
in the near term. Supply is likely to remain constrained while high
mortgage rates discourage mobility. And mortgage rates are apt
to remain elevated amid firm economic growth expectations and
heavy government and corporate debt issuance.

### Page 3

Economics Week Ahead Economics
CPI • Friday
Headline inflation looks to have picked up last month. We estimate
the CPI rose 0.40% in August, with gasoline prices increasing a little
over 4% as renewed tensions in the Middle East pushed oil higher.
Grocery prices also likely rebounded after modestly declining in July.
Outside of food and energy, price pressures appear little changed.
We expect the core CPI to rise 0.23%, essentially matching July's

pace. Core goods inflation should remain firm, led by another solid
increase in used vehicle prices as consumer prices continue to
catch up to the trend indicated at wholesale auctions. Meantime,
household furnishings and other tariff-sensitive categories appear
to be stabilizing after declining earlier this year.
Core services inflation should be little changed with another 0.2%
monthly increase. But underneath the surface, we expect a modest
pickup in travel (led by lodging away from home) to offset slightly

cooler medical care and housing cost growth.
All told, we expect the report to show that while headline inflation
remains influenced by the ebbs and flows of the conflict in the
Middle East, core inflation remains contained. Should our forecast
prove correct, core CPI will ease to 2.4% year-over-year, in line with
its 6-month annualized rate but above the more recent 3-month
annualized pace of 1.7%.

### Page 4

Weekly Economics
G10 Week Ahead
European Central Bank Monetary Policy Meeting •
Thursday
We expect the European Central Bank (ECB) to raise the Deposit
Rate by 25 bps to 2.50% next week. August headline inflation
remained elevated at 3.3% year-over-year, though softer core
and services inflation suggest underlying pressures continue to
moderate. Still, the more hawkish tone of the July minutes and
renewed rise in energy prices should keep policymakers cautious.

The recent escalation in the Middle East has pushed fuel prices
at the pump close to their 2022 peaks, while natural gas storage
remains below historical levels, leaving the Eurozone vulnerable
to further energy price increases. The ECB is unlikely to respond
aggressively if the shock remains confined to energy given the
downside risks to growth. However, a broader rise in food inflation
linked to El Niño would be more concerning, as highly visible prices

could lift inflation expectations and generate second-round effects.
For now, resilient Q3 activity, elevated headline inflation and
contained wage growth support one final hike. The ECB will likely
retain and emphasize its scenario-based outlook in the updated
staff projections given continued uncertainty around the conflict
and energy prices. The policy path beyond September will depend
on whether energy and food pressures persist and begin to spread,

which could leave room for further tightening.

U.K. Monthly GDP • Friday
We expect UK GDP to rise 0.2% month-over-month in July, with
growth of 0.3% on a three-month-over-three-month basis.

Activity likely moderated after June’s strong rebound, though
services remained supportive as the July services PMI rose to 52.1.
Retail sales volumes fell 0.5% month-over-month, while softer
manufacturing activity likely reflected some reversal of earlier
precautionary stockbuilding.
The moderation appears more consistent with payback from
June than a broader deterioration in momentum. August PMIs
remain consistent with Q3 GDP growth of around 0.3% quarter-

over-quarter. Still, the renewed escalation in the Middle East and
associated rise in gas and energy prices adds upside risk to inflation
and could weigh on household demand. Against this backdrop and
with Bank of England hawks continuing to emphasize the need for
tighter policy, we expect the BoE to remain cautious and continue
to look for a rate hike in Q4. -1.0%

### Page 5

Economics Week Ahead Economics
EM Week Ahead
Mexico CPI • Wednesday
We expect Mexico’s August inflation rate to edge higher, with
headline CPI rising to 3.34% year-over-year (0.27% month-over-
month) from 3.26% year-over-year in early August. Part of the
pickup likely reflects less favorable base effects and a smaller
disinflationary contribution from food prices. While agricultural
prices continued to help restrain inflation in mid-August, the drag

was notably less pronounced than in July. Core inflation should
remain elevated at 3.98% year-over-year (0.25% month-over-
month).
While inflation remains within Banxico’s midpoint 3% target,
persistent core and services inflation suggest convergence toward
the midpoint of the target could prove gradual. For now, we
continue to expect Banxico to keep rates on hold, with the policy
rate remaining at 6.50% through the rest of 2026 and into early
2027 as policymakers await clearer evidence that underlying

inflation pressures are easing sustainably. While rate cuts remain
a possibility in 2027, the current inflation backdrop argues for
patience in the near term.

Brazil CPI • Friday
We expect Brazil’s August inflation rate to slow to 4.20% year-over-
year, helped by lower electricity, food and fuel prices. The main
driver should be a temporary reduction in residential electricity bills
stemming from a one-off credit, while recent data already point to
a notable easing in price pressures. Mid-August CPI came in weaker
than expected, with inflation falling to 4.24% year-over-year amid
declines in electricity, food, fuel and airfare prices. Beyond these

temporary factors, restrictive monetary policy continues to weigh
on economic activity, as evidenced by the recent slowdown in Q2
GDP growth. However, underlying inflation risks remain. A still-
tight labor market and government measures aimed at supporting
consumption could keep services inflation sticky, making progress
toward the 3.0% inflation target more gradual.
Overall, the inflation outlook remains consistent with additional
policy easing. We expect COPOM to deliver a 25 bps rate cut in

September, bringing the Selic Rate to 13.75%. Beyond September,
policymakers are likely to proceed cautiously as they assess
incoming inflation data and the impact of the election cycle on
inflation expectations. Our base case remains for a pause after
September, with additional easing more likely in 2027.

### Page 4

◆ The economy remains on the recovery track despite weakness in some
areas
Annualized real GDP growth in the second quarter was +1.1% QoQ, with growth
staying in positive territory for three straight quarters. Although personal
consumption exhibited weakness and capital investment declined, overall growth
was lifted by foreign demand. Exports, particularly of services, increased, and the
drop in imports of Middle Eastern crude oil also helped foreign demand turn positive.

◆ Housing starts are weak
Housing starts surged 18.6% YoY in June. They have continued to rise on a YoY
basis, but the jump was mainly due to a rebound from the slump in the same month
of the previous year. The recovery in the number of starts is actually moderate,
reflecting higher interest rates and soaring construction costs.
◆ Earthquake impact will need to be watched
Damage from the earthquake that occurred in Kumamoto Prefecture at the end of

July is weighing on economic activity in the affected areas. Although moves toward
recovery can be seen, with many companies having already resumed operations,
the impact could become prolonged in the following scenarios:
The first is if damage to capital stock such as factories and roads has been severe.
Some companies have sustained damage to production facilities, making an early
resumption of operations difficult in some cases.
The second is if a downturn in tourism demand occurs. According to some media

reports, there has been an uptick in cancellations of accommodation reservations in
Kumamoto Prefecture and neighboring prefectures since the earthquake. After the
2016 Kumamoto earthquake, the decline in travel demand from Japanese visitors
was short lived, but lower demand from foreign tourists persisted for a long period.
Annualized real GDP growth was +1.1% quarter over quarter (QoQ) in Q2 2026

### Page 5

Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026
The Japan Research Institute, Limited
Exports and inbound demand remain solid despite weakness in some areas
◆ Goods exports are on the verge of picking up
Goods exports are treading water. A sharp rise in memory chip prices is among
factors pushing up export prices, resulting in record high levels of exports in value
terms recently. On the other hand, the increase in volume terms is limited. One

possible reason is that Japan's export competitiveness has declined in fields such as
semiconductors, where global demand is growing.
Looking ahead, exports are expected to pick up. Capital investment by chipmakers
is expanding worldwide, and this should lift exports of semiconductor manufacturing
equipment, an area in which Japan has strength. However, continued caution is
needed regarding the effects of prolonged turmoil in the Middle East. A global

slowdown due to elevated crude oil prices could weigh on exports.
◆ Reluctance to visit among Chinese travelers is weighing on demand for
travel to Japan
Inbound demand is currently soft. The Chinese government's advisory urging its
citizens to refrain from traveling to Japan is having an adverse impact. The number
of Chinese visitors to Japan has fallen to about half the level prior to the advisory.
Meanwhile, the number of tourists from countries other than China remains firm.

Inbound demand is expected to stay robust going forward. Continued increases in
tourists from countries other than China will remain a supporting factor. The
expectation that the yen will remain weak will also act to push up inbound
consumption. However, there are two downside risks going forward:
The first is that the Chinese government keeps the advisory in place for a
prolonged period. When China banned group travel to South Korea in 2017, tourist

numbers stayed low for two to three years.
The second is the impact of higher crude oil prices. Major airlines have embarked
on phased increases in fuel surcharges, which could weigh on inbound demand
during the summer.
